Jackson was ordered to pay \$100,000 in penalties.[^cite_note-sonydeal-244]

In mid-2006, Jackson moved to [Grouse Lodge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grouse_Lodge), a residential recording studio near [Rosemount, County Westmeath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosemount,_County_Westmeath), Ireland. There, he began work on a new album with the American producers [will.i.am](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will.i.am) and [Rodney Jerkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodney_Jerkins).[^cite_note-302] That November, Jackson invited an *[Access Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Access_Hollywood)* camera crew into the studio in Westmeath.[^cite_note-Achievements-174] He briefly joined a performance of "We Are the World" at the [World Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_World_Music_Awards) in London, which served as his last public performance, and accepted the Diamond Award for sales of 100 million records.[^cite_note-Achievements-174][^cite_note-303] Jackson returned to the US in December, settling in Las Vegas. That month, he attended [James Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Brown)'s funeral in [Augusta, Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augusta,_Georgia), where he gave a eulogy calling Brown his greatest inspiration.[^cite_note-304]

A view from above of a large property in a semi-desert. The landscape is pale with clumps of vegetation. The property shows circular structures between the buildings.

An aerial view of part of Jackson's 2,800-acre (11 km

2

) [Neverland Valley Ranch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neverland_Valley_Ranch) near Los Olivos, California, showing the rides

Jackson and Sony bought another music publishing company during 2007, [Famous Music LLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Famous_Music), formerly owned by [Viacom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viacom_\(2005%E2%80%932019\)). The deal gave him the rights to songs by [Eminem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eminem) and [Beck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beck), among others.[^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306] Jackson mentioned having no regrets about his career despite the "deliberate attempts to hurt me".[^cite_note-307] That March, he visited a US Army post in Japan, [Camp Zama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_Zama), to greet more than 3,000 troops and their families.[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309] By September, Jackson was still working on his next album,[^cite_note-310] with [Kanye West](523032.md) joining as a producer.[^cite_note-311]

In 2008, for the 25th anniversary of *Thriller*, Jackson and Sony released *[Thriller 25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thriller_25)*, a [reissue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reissue) which included five newly remixed tracks co-produced by him and guest producers, including the singles: "[The Girl Is Mine 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Girl_Is_Mine_2008)" and "[Wanna Be Startin' Somethin' 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wanna_Be_Startin'_Somethin'_2008)". *Thriller 25* was the last recorded work released during Jackson's lifetime that he was extensively involved in.[^cite_note-312] For Jackson's 50th birthday, Sony BMG released a series of greatest hits albums, *[King of Pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_Pop_\(album\))*, with different tracklists for different regions.[^cite_note-313] That July, Fortress Investments threatened to [foreclose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreclose) on Neverland Ranch, which he had used as collateral for his loans. Fortress sold Jackson's debts to [Colony Capital LLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colony_Capital_LLC).[^cite_note-314] In November, Jackson transferred Neverland Ranch's title to Sycamore Valley Ranch Company LLC, a joint venture between Jackson and Colony Capital LLC. The deal earned him \$35 million.[^cite_note-315] In 2009, Jackson arranged to sell a collection of his memorabilia of more than 1,000 items through [Julien's Auction House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julien's_Auctions), but canceled the auction in April.[^cite_note-316]

In March 2009, amid speculation about his finances and health, Jackson announced a series of comeback concerts, [This Is It](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/This_Is_It_\(concert_residency\)), at a press conference at [the O2 Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_O2_Arena).[^cite_note-Adams-2009-317] The shows were to be his first major concerts since the HIStory World Tour in 1997. Jackson suggested he would retire after the shows. The initial plan was for ten concerts in London, followed by shows in Paris, New York City and Mumbai. Randy Phillips, the president and chief executive of [AEG Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AEG_Live), predicted the first ten dates would earn Jackson £50 million.[^cite_note-318]

The London residency was increased to fifty dates after record-breaking ticket sales; more than one million were sold in less than two hours.[^cite_note-319] The concerts were to run from July 13, 2009, to March 6, 2010. Jackson moved to Los Angeles, where he rehearsed in the weeks leading up to the tour under the direction of the choreographer [Kenny Ortega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenny_Ortega), whom he had worked with during his previous tours. Rehearsals took place at [the Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kia_Forum) and the [Staples Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staples_Center) owned by AEG.[^cite_note-320] By this point, Jackson's debt had grown to almost \$500 million. By the time of his death, he was three or four months behind payments on his home in San Fernando Valley.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] *[The Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Independent)* reported that Jackson planned a string of further ventures designed to recoup his debts, including a world tour, a new album, films, a museum and a casino.[^cite_note-Adams-2009-317]

## Death

Main article: [Death of Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Michael_Jackson)

Jackson's Star with flowers and notes on it

Fans placed flowers and notes on Jackson's star on the Hollywood Walk of Fame on the day of his death.

On June 25, 2009, less than three weeks before his concert residency was scheduled to begin in London, with all dates sold out, Jackson died at age 50 from [cardiac arrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiac_arrest) caused by a [propofol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Propofol) and [benzodiazepine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benzodiazepine) overdose.[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-CNN-324] His personal physician, [Conrad Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conrad_Murray), had administered various medications to help him sleep at his home in [Holmby Hills, Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holmby_Hills,_Los_Angeles). Paramedics received a [911](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/911_\(emergency_telephone_number\)) call at 12\:21 pm [Pacific time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_time) (19\:21 UTC) and arrived four minutes later.[^cite_note-325] Jackson was not breathing, and [CPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CPR) was performed.[^cite_note-326] Resuscitation efforts continued en route to [Ronald Reagan UCLA Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Reagan_UCLA_Medical_Center) and for more than an hour after his arrival, but were unsuccessful,[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-Times-328] and he was pronounced dead at 2\:26 pm Pacific time (21\:26 UTC).[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330] Murray had administered propofol, [lorazepam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorazepam), and [midazolam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midazolam);[^cite_note-331] Jackson's death resulted from a propofol overdose.[^cite_note-CNN-324][^cite_note-Times-328]



Jackson's childhood home in Gary, Indiana, one month after his death, showing floral tributes

News of his death spread rapidly online, causing websites to slow down or [crash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crash_\(computing\)) from heavy traffic,[^cite_note-332] and placing unprecedented strain[^cite_note-Wood-333] on services including [Google](1092923.md),[^cite_note-Shiels-334] [AOL Instant Messenger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AOL_Instant_Messenger),[^cite_note-Wood-333] [Twitter](9988187.md),[^cite_note-Shiels-334] and [Wikipedia](5043734.md).[^cite_note-Shiels-334] Overall [web traffic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_traffic) increased by between 11% and 20%.[^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336] MTV and [BET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BET) aired [marathons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathon_\(media\)) of Jackson's music videos.[^cite_note-337] MTV briefly returned to its original music video format,[^cite_note-Barnes-13] broadcasting extended blocks of his videos alongside live news specials featuring reactions from MTV personalities and other celebrities.[^cite_note-338] President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) sent a letter of condolence to the Jackson family,[^cite_note-339] and the [House of Representatives](19468510.md) observed a [moment of silence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moment_of_silence).[^cite_note-340]

### Memorial service

Main article: [Michael Jackson memorial service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson_memorial_service)

A large pink/cream-colored box in front of a stained glass window.

Jackson's unmarked crypt at the end of the Sanctuary of Ascension in the Holly Terrace of the [Great Mausoleum, Forest Lawn Glendale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Lawn_Memorial_Park_\(Glendale\))

A group of people standing outside a gated area. There are trees, bushes, and grassed areas. A majority of the area the people and in are shadowed by the trees by the gate.

Fans visiting the makeshift memorial set up outside the Neverland Ranch entrance shortly after Jackson's death

Jackson's memorial was held on July 7, 2009, at the [Staples Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staples_Center) in Los Angeles, preceded by a private family service at [Forest Lawn Memorial Park's Hall of Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Lawn_Memorial_Park,_Hollywood_Hills#Court_of_Liberty). Over 1.6 million fans applied for tickets to the memorial; the 8,750 recipients were drawn at random, and each received two tickets.[^cite_note-341] The memorial service was one of the most watched events in [streaming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streaming) history,[^cite_note-342] with an estimated US audience of 31.1 million[^cite_note-343] and a worldwide audience of an estimated 2.5 to 3 billion.[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345]

[Mariah Carey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariah_Carey), [Stevie Wonder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stevie_Wonder), [Lionel Richie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lionel_Richie), [Jennifer Hudson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Hudson), and [Shaheen Jafargholi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaheen_Jafargholi) performed at the memorial, and [Berry Gordy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berry_Gordy), [Brooke Shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooke_Shields), [Smokey Robinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smokey_Robinson) and [Queen Latifah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Latifah) gave eulogies.[^cite_note-346] [Al Sharpton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Sharpton) received a standing ovation with cheers when he told Jackson's children: "Wasn't nothing strange about your daddy. It was strange what your daddy had to deal with. But he dealt with it anyway."[^cite_note-347] Jackson's 11-year-old daughter [Paris Katherine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Jackson), speaking publicly for the first time, wept as she addressed the crowd.[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349] Lucious Smith provided a closing prayer.[^cite_note-350] On September 3, 2009, Jackson was entombed at [Forest Lawn Memorial Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Lawn_Memorial_Park_\(Glendale\)) in [Glendale, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glendale,_California).[^cite_note-351]

### Criminal investigation and prosecution of Conrad Murray

Main article: [*People v. Murray*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_v._Murray)

In August 2009, the [Los Angeles County Coroner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Coroner) ruled that Jackson's death was a [homicide](123451.md).[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353] Law enforcement officials charged Murray with [involuntary manslaughter](7397035.md) on February 8, 2010.[^cite_note-354] In late 2011, he was found guilty of the charge[^cite_note-355] and held without bail to await sentencing.[^cite_note-356] Murray was sentenced to four years in prison, but was released after one year and eleven months.[^cite_note-357]

### Posthumous releases and productions

Jackson's posthumous releases and productions are administered by the [estate of Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estate_of_Michael_Jackson), which owns Jackson's trademarks and rights to his name, image and likeness.[^cite_note-358] The first posthumous Jackson song, "[This Is It](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/This_Is_It_\(Michael_Jackson_song\))", co-written in the 1980s with [Paul Anka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Anka), was released in October 2009. The surviving Jackson brothers reunited to record backing vocals.[^cite_note-359] It was followed by a documentary film about the rehearsals for the canceled This Is It tour, *[Michael Jackson's This Is It](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson's_This_Is_It)*,[^cite_note-360] and [a compilation album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson's_This_Is_It_\(album\)).[^cite_note-361] Despite a limited two-week engagement, the film became the highest-grossing documentary or concert film ever, with earnings of more than \$260 million worldwide.[^cite_note-362] Jackson's estate received 90% of the profits.[^cite_note-363]

In late 2010, Sony released the first posthumous album, *[Michael](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_\(Michael_Jackson_album\))*, and the lead single "[Hold My Hand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hold_My_Hand_\(Michael_Jackson_and_Akon_song\))", a duet with [Akon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akon). The Jackson collaborator will.i.am expressed disgust, saying that Jackson would not have approved the release.[^cite_note-RS:_will.i.am-364] Controversy ensued over the authenticiy of Jackson's vocals on the tracks "[Breaking News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breaking_News_\(song\))", "[Keep Your Head Up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keep_Your_Head_Up_\(Michael_Jackson_song\))", and "[Monster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monster_\(Michael_Jackson_song\))" (featuring [50 Cent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/50_Cent)).[^cite_note-Billboard:_MichaelSongsRemoved-365] Members of Jackson's family, will.i.am, and fans alleged they were performed by an impersonator.[^cite_note-RS:_will.i.am-364][^cite_note-366] In 2022, Sony and the Jackson estate reissued *Michael* without the disputed tracks, stating it was "time to move beyond the distraction surrounding them".[^cite_note-Billboard:_MichaelSongsRemoved-365]

The video game developer [Ubisoft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ubisoft) released a [music game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_game) featuring Jackson for the 2010 holiday season, *[Michael Jackson: The Experience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson:_The_Experience)*. It was among the first games to use [Kinect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinect) and [PlayStation Move](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PlayStation_Move), the motion-detecting camera systems for [Xbox 360](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xbox_360) and [PlayStation 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PlayStation_3).[^cite_note-367] In October 2011, the theater company [Cirque du Soleil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cirque_du_Soleil) launched *[Michael Jackson: The Immortal World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson:_The_Immortal_World_Tour)*, a \$57 million production in Montreal.[^cite_note-368] A larger and more theatrical Cirque show, *[Michael Jackson: One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson:_One)*, designed for residency at the [Mandalay Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandalay_Bay) resort in Las Vegas, opened on May 23, 2013, in a renovated theater.[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370]

In 2012, in an attempt to end a family dispute, Jackson's brother Jermaine retracted his signature on a public letter criticizing executors of Jackson's estate and his mother's advisors over the legitimacy of his brother's will.[^cite_note-371] T.J. Jackson, the son of Tito Jackson, was given co-guardianship of Michael Jackson's children after false reports of Katherine Jackson going missing.[^cite_note-372] *[Xscape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xscape_\(album\))*, an album of unreleased material, was released on May 13, 2014.[^cite_note-373] The lead single, a duet between Jackson and [Justin Timberlake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Timberlake), "[Love Never Felt So Good](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Never_Felt_So_Good)", reached number 9 on the US *Billboard* Hot 100, making Jackson the first artist to have a top-10 single on the chart in five different decades.[^cite_note-374]

Later in 2014, [Queen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_\(band\)) released a duet recorded with Jackson in the 1980s.[^cite_note-Greene-69] A compilation album, *[Scream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scream_\(Michael_Jackson_album\))*, was released on September 29, 2017.[^cite_note-375] A [jukebox musical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jukebox_musical), *[MJ the Musical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MJ_the_Musical)*, premiered on Broadway in 2022.[^cite_note-376] [Myles Frost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myles_Frost) won the 2022 [Tony Award for Best Actor in a Musical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Award_for_Best_Actor_in_a_Musical) for his portrayal of Jackson.[^cite_note-377] On November 18, 2022, Epic released a [40th-anniversary edition reissue of *Thriller*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thriller_40).[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] Since Jackson's death, his estate has grossed \$2 billion in ticket revenue from *MJ the Musical*, *[Michael Jackson's This Is It](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson's_This_Is_It)* and two Cirque du Soleil productions.[^cite_note-380] A biographical film, *[Michael](70014183.md)*, directed by [Antoine Fuqua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antoine_Fuqua) and starring Jackson's nephew [Jaafar Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaafar_Jackson), premiered in April 2026.[^cite_note-381] It received generally negative reviews from critics.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] The film broke records for biopics on opening day and opening week, with an opening weekend gross estimated at \$97 million domestically and \$217 million worldwide, becoming [the second highest-grossing film of 2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_in_film#Highest-grossing_films).[^cite_note-384]

### Posthumous sales

At the [2009 American Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_American_Music_Awards), Jackson won four posthumous awards, including two for his compilation album *[Number Ones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Number_Ones_\(Michael_Jackson_album\))*, bringing his total American Music Awards to 26.[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386] In the year after his death, more than 16.1 million copies of Jackson's albums were sold in the US alone, and 35 million copies were sold worldwide, more than any other artist in 2009.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388] He became the first artist to sell one million [music downloads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_download) in a week, with 2.6 million song downloads. *Thriller*, *Number Ones* and *[The Essential Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Essential_Michael_Jackson)* became the first catalog albums to outsell any new album.[^cite_note-389] Jackson also became the first artist to have four of the top-20 best-selling albums in a single year in the US.[^cite_note-390] Within the year following his death, Jackson sold over 75 million records worldwide.[^cite_note-391] By the end of 2013, Jackson had sold over 50 million albums worldwide since his death.[^cite_note-392]

Following the surge in sales, in March 2010, Sony Music signed a \$250 million deal (equivalent to \$370 million in 2025) with the Jackson estate to extend their distribution rights to Jackson's back catalog until at least 2017; it had been due to expire in 2015. It was the [most expensive music contract](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_music_deals) for a single artist in history.[^cite_note-Sony-393][^cite_note-394] They agreed to release ten albums of previously unreleased material and new collections of released work.[^cite_note-Sony-393][^cite_note-395] The deal was extended in 2017.[^cite_note-396] That July, a Los Angeles court awarded [Quincy Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quincy_Jones) \$9.4 million of disputed royalty payments for *Off the Wall*, *Thriller*, and *Bad*.[^cite_note-Who's_Bad-57] In July 2018, Sony/ATV bought the estate's stake in EMI for \$287.5 million.[^cite_note-397]

In 2015, *Thriller* became the first album to be certified for 30 million shipments in the US.[^cite_note-398] A year later, it was certified 33-times platinum after [Soundscan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soundscan) added streams and audio downloads to album certifications.[^cite_note-399][^cite_note-401] In February 2024, Sony Music acquired half of Jackson's publishing rights and recording masters for \$600 million. The deal includes assets from his [Mijac publishing catalog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mijac_Music), but excludes royalties from several Jackson-related productions, such as the *MJ* Broadway musical and the 2026 *[Michael](70014183.md)* film. The transaction is possibly the largest for a single musician's work.[^cite_note-402][^cite_note-403]

### Posthumous child sexual abuse allegations

In 2013, the choreographer [Wade Robson](654142.md) filed a lawsuit alleging that Jackson had sexually abused him for seven years, beginning when he was seven years old (1989–1996).[^cite_note-404] In 2014, a case was filed by James Safechuck, alleging similar sexual abuse over a four-year period starting when Safechuck was ten (1988–1992).[^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407] Both had previously testified in Jackson's defense during the 1993 allegations; Robson did so again in 2005.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409] In 2015, Robson's case against Jackson's estate was dismissed as it exceeded California's [statute of limitations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statute_of_limitations). Safechuck's claim was also time-barred.[^cite_note-410]

In 2017, the [Los Angeles County Superior Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Superior_Court) ruled that Jackson's corporations could not be held accountable for his alleged past actions.[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412] The rulings were appealed. On October 20, 2020, Safechuck's lawsuit against Jackson's corporations was again dismissed. The judge ruled that there was no evidence that Safechuck had a relationship with Jackson's corporation, nor was it proven that there was a special relationship between the two.[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414][^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416] On April 26, 2021, Robson's case was dismissed because of a lack of supporting evidence that the defendants exercised control over Jackson.[^cite_note-417]

In 2020, California law was modified to allow plaintiffs in child sex abuse cases additional time to file lawsuits. In October 2020 and again in April 2021, the Los Angeles County Superior Court ruled in *Safechuck v. MJJ Productions* that MJJ Productions Inc. and MJJ Ventures Inc. employees were not legally obligated to protect the two men from Jackson. In August 2023, California's Second District Court of Appeal overturned the ruling, and the case was approved to move forward to [trial court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_court).[^cite_note-418] The trial is set to begin in November 2026.[^cite_note-419]

In 2019, four siblings, Edward, Dominic, Marie-Nicole and Aldo Cascio, alleged that Jackson had abused them when they were children. They had defended Jackson against previous allegations, but changed their position after the release of *Leaving Neverland*. The estate paid them a \$3.5 million settlement without admitting wrongdoing. In 2026, the Cascio siblings filed a federal lawsuit against the Jackson estate, saying they had been coerced into the settlement. On August 12, a federal judge dismissed the lawsuit, as a clause in the settlement specified the dispute required [arbitration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arbitration_in_the_United_States) and could not be handled in a public court.[^cite_note-420]

#### *Leaving Neverland*

Main article: [Leaving Neverland](59625891.md)

Robson and Safechuck's allegations were the subject of [Dan Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Reed_\(director\))'s documentary film *[Leaving Neverland](59625891.md)*, broadcast by HBO in March 2019.[^cite_note-421] The documentary triggered a backlash against Jackson;[^cite_note-422] radio stations in New Zealand, Canada, the UK and the Netherlands removed his music from their playlists,[^cite_note-423][^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425] and a 50th anniversary documentary about the Jackson 5 was canceled.[^cite_note-Landrum-2019-426] However, Jackson's album sales increased,[^cite_note-427] and his honors were not rescinded.[^cite_note-Landrum-2019-426] The *Billboard* senior editor Gail Mitchell said she and a colleague interviewed about thirty music executives who believed Jackson's legacy could withstand the controversy.[^cite_note-428]

Jackson's family condemned *Leaving Neverland* as a "public lynching",[^cite_note-429] and the Jackson estate called it a "tabloid character assassination \[Jackson] endured in life, and now in death".[^cite_note-430] Close associates of Jackson, such as [Corey Feldman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corey_Feldman), [Aaron Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Carter), Brett Barnes, and [Macaulay Culkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macaulay_Culkin), said that he had never molested them,[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433] and rebuttal documentaries, such as *[Square One: Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Square_One:_Michael_Jackson)*, *[Neverland Firsthand: Investigating the Michael Jackson Documentary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neverland_Firsthand:_Investigating_the_Michael_Jackson_Documentary)* and *[Michael Jackson: Chase the Truth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson:_Chase_the_Truth)*, sought to refute the allegations in *Leaving Neverland*.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436] In late 2019, some New Zealand and Canadian radio stations re-added Jackson's music to their playlists due to "positive listener survey results".[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438] *[Leaving Neverland 2: Surviving Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leaving_Neverland_2:_Surviving_Michael_Jackson)* (2025), which covers Robson and Safechuck taking their allegations to trial, premiered on [YouTube](3524766.md).[^cite_note-439]

## Legacy

Main article: [Cultural impact of Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_Michael_Jackson)

See also: [List of Michael Jackson records and achievements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Michael_Jackson_records_and_achievements)



Jackson at the [White House](33057.md) in 1984. President [Ronald Reagan](25433.md) described him as "one of the most talented, most popular and most exciting superstars."

[^cite_note-440]

Jackson has been referred to as the "[King of Pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honorific_nicknames_in_popular_music)" for having transformed the art of music videos and paving the way for modern pop music. For much of Jackson's career, he had an unparalleled worldwide influence over the younger generation.[^cite_note-441] His influence extended beyond the music industry; he impacted dance, led fashion trends, and raised awareness for global affairs.[^cite_note-442] Jackson's music and videos fostered racial diversity in MTV's roster and steered its focus from rock to pop music and R\&B, leading to the discontinuation of the [album-oriented rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Album-oriented_rock) format previously dominant on the channel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung200925-44][^cite_note-443] In songs such as "[Black or White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_or_White)", "[Heal the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heal_the_World)", "[Earth Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earth_Song)" and "[They Don't Care About Us](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/They_Don't_Care_About_Us)", Jackson's music emphasized [racial integration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_integration) and [environmentalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmentalism) and protested injustice.[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445] He is considered one of the most significant cultural figures of the 20th century,[^cite_note-446] and his contributions to music, dance, and fashion, along with his publicized personal life, made him a global figure in popular culture for over four decades.[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450]

&#x20;Jackson's meteoric rise in the 1980s catapulted him to global stardom, and his influence transcended borders. In Africa, his influence was compared to [Nelson Mandela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson_Mandela) for his ability to inspire and unite diverse audiences through his music.[^cite_note-ReutersImpact-451][^cite_note-452] Similarly, in India, Jackson was likened to [Mahatma Gandhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahatma_Gandhi), with his art championing themes of social justice and [humanitarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanitarianism),[^cite_note-453] and his influence extending to [Bollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollywood) where it was said that anyone who danced well was compared to Jackson.[^cite_note-ReutersImpact-451] In Europe, Jackson's influence was compared to that of the [Pope](23056.md); huge crowds gathered to see him in public and even more attended his concerts, which spread messages of love and healing during rough economic and political times.[^cite_note-454] His presence in the Middle East was considered as widespread as [Coca-Cola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola), symbolizing a shared global culture through his music.[^cite_note-455] Additionally, Jackson is considered the backbone of the [K-pop](629945.md) industry in South Korea, influencing countless artists and shaping the genre's development.[^cite_note-456][^cite_note-457]

> So vast, far-reaching and was his impact—particularly in the wake of *[Thriller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thriller_\(album\))*'s colossal and heretofore unmatched commercial success—that there weren't a whole lot of artists who *weren't* trying to mimic some of the Jackson formula.

— J. Edward Keyes of *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone)*[^cite_note-458]

[Danyel Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danyel_Smith), chief content officer of Vibe Media Group and the editor-in-chief of *[Vibe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vibe_\(magazine\))*, described Jackson as "the greatest star".[^cite_note-459] Steve Huey of [AllMusic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AllMusic) called him "an unstoppable juggernaut, possessed of all the skills to dominate the charts seemingly at will: an instantly identifiable voice, eye-popping dance moves, stunning musical versatility and loads of sheer star power".[^cite_note-allmusic-12] BET said Jackson was "quite simply the greatest entertainer of all time" whose "sound, style, movement and legacy continues to inspire artists of all genres".[^cite_note-460] In 1984, *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* pop critic [Jay Cocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jay_Cocks) wrote that Jackson was the biggest phenomenon since the Beatles and Elvis Presley, while possibly "the most popular black singer ever". Cocks declared him a "star of records, radio, rock video" and the "dancer with the fanciest feet".[^cite_note-Time-89] In 2003, *[The Daily Telegraph](23797577.md)* writer [Tom Utley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Utley) described Jackson as "extremely important" and a "genius".[^cite_note-461] At Jackson's memorial service on July 7, 2009, Motown founder Berry Gordy called Jackson "the greatest entertainer that ever lived".[^cite_note-462][^cite_note-463] Jackson is recognized as the "Most Successful Entertainer of All Time" by *[Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records)*.[^cite_note-CityNews-464][^cite_note-Ditzian-465] He has also appeared on *Rolling Stone*'s lists of the "Greatest Singers of All Time".[^cite_note-100_Greatest-466][^cite_note-467]

Craig Glenday, the editor-in-chief of *Guinness World Records*, called Jackson the most famous person in the world in 2006. Following Jackson's death, Glenday wrote in an obituary that Jackson had maintained this status up until his death,[^cite_note-468] later remarking that his fame had exceeded that of [Confucius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucius).[^cite_note-469] *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* wrote that he was in a league of his own in terms of fame, noting that Jackson had become so famous that the number of people who might not know who he was had become statistically insignificant.[^cite_note-470] Due to his unprecedented influence, Jackson is widely recognized as one of the most famous and globally renowned figures in history.[^cite_note-471] Reports of his fame extend from the Middle East,[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-473] Africa,[^cite_note-ReutersImpact-451][^cite_note-474] India,[^cite_note-475][^cite_note-476] and China[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478] to tribes in the Amazon.[^cite_note-479] His influence even reached remote corners of the world such as [São Tomé and Príncipe](20026306.md), or [Tristan da Cunha](31361.md), where tribute artists keep his legacy alive by celebrating his music.[^cite_note-480][^cite_note-481]



The Jackson wax figure at [Madame Tussauds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_Tussauds) in London

In 2018, the [National Portrait Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Portrait_Gallery,_London) named Jackson the most depicted cultural figure of the century,[^cite_note-482] later stating that Jackson's influence on art rivaled that of [Jesus Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus_Christ).[^cite_note-483] [Nicholas Cullinan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_Cullinan), director of the National Portrait Gallery and curator of the *[Michael Jackson: On the Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson:_On_the_Wall)* exhibition, described Jackson's influence on art as unprecedented and claimed in 2018 that Jackson was the most depicted figure in the history of [contemporary art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_art).[^cite_note-484] In 2014, a comprehensive study conducted by researcher Young-Ho Eom at the [University of Toulouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Toulouse) identified Jackson as one of the most influential people of all time.[^cite_note-485] The study utilized advanced ranking methods, including 2D Rank and PageRank algorithms, to analyze the influence of historical figures. Jackson was prominently placed on the list of top influencers, alongside Swedish botanist [Carl Linnaeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Linnaeus), [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md), and Jesus Christ. Another study conducted in 2013 also identified Jackson as one of the most influential people of all time. This study ranked Jackson at the top of the list, alongside [Napoleon Bonaparte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon_Bonaparte), highlighting the extraordinary influence and global recognition that Jackson achieved throughout his career.[^cite_note-486] Numerous publications and academic studies[^cite_note-487] have noted his influence beyond music in fields such as psychology, law, chemistry, and engineering.[^cite_note-488][^cite_note-489][^cite_note-490]

## Philanthropy

Main article: [Philanthropy of Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philanthropy_of_Michael_Jackson)

President [Ronald Reagan](25433.md) rewarding Jackson in 1984 for his support of alcohol and drug abuse charities

Jackson is well known for his prolific philanthropy and humanitarianism.[^cite_note-Daunt-491][^cite_note-492][^cite_note-493][^cite_note-Humanitarian-96] Jackson's early charitable work has been described by *[The Chronicle of Philanthropy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Chronicle_of_Philanthropy)* as having "paved the way for the current surge in celebrity philanthropy",[^cite_note-494] and by the *[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times)* as having "set the standard for generosity for other entertainers".[^cite_note-Daunt-491] By some estimates, he donated over \$500 million, not accounting for inflation, to various charities over the course of his life.[^cite_note-Daunt-491] In 1992, Jackson established his [Heal the World Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heal_the_World_Foundation), to which he donated several million dollars in revenue from his [Dangerous World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dangerous_World_Tour).[^cite_note-495]

Jackson's philanthropic activities went beyond just monetary donations. He also performed at [benefit concerts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benefit_concert), some of which he arranged.[^cite_note-496] He gifted tickets for his regular concert performances to groups that assist underprivileged children.[^cite_note-497] Jackson visited sick children in hospitals around the world.[^cite_note-498] He donated valuable, personal and professional paraphernalia for numerous charity auctions. He received various awards and accolades for his philanthropic work, including two bestowed by American presidents. The vast breadth of Jackson's philanthropic work has earned recognition in the *[Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records)*.[^cite_note-Daunt-491][^cite_note-499][^cite_note-Guinness-500]

On May 14, 1984, President [Ronald Reagan](25433.md) gave Jackson an award recognizing his support of alcohol and drug abuse charities,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2009304–307-501] and in recognition of his support for the [Ad Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ad_Council)'s and the [National Highway Traffic Safety Administration](989530.md)'s Drunk Driving Prevention campaign. Jackson allowed the campaign to use "Beat It" for its public service announcements.[^cite_note-502]

## Artistry

### Vocal style



Jackson performing in [Vienna](55866.md), Austria, 1988

Jackson was known as an expressive vocalist with [absolute pitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absolute_pitch).[^cite_note-503][^cite_note-504][^cite_note-505] Critics describe his vocal theatricality as having a range from clear and soft to harsh and aggressive,[^cite_note-506][^cite_note-507][^cite_note-508][^cite_note-509] depending on the genre sung. Jackson is the only recipient to ever receive [Grammy Awards](44635.md) for three different genres of vocal performance as a soloist, winning [Pop Vocal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Male_Pop_Vocal_Performance) for "[Thriller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thriller_\(song\))", [Rock Vocal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Male_Rock_Vocal_Performance) for "[Beat It](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beat_It)", and [Rhythm and Blues Vocal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Male_R\&B_Vocal_Performance) with both "[Billie Jean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billie_Jean)" and "[Don't Stop 'Til You Get Enough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don't_Stop_'Til_You_Get_Enough)". [Bruce Swedien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Swedien), his long-time sound engineer, emphasized that a critical element of their vocal recordings are of him and Jackson trying numerous approaches to rhythmic, emotional and technical distinctions to consummate a "sonic character".[^cite_note-510] His stylings, such as common use of [staccato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staccato), [legato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legato), [falsetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falsetto) as well as [vocal hiccups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocal_hiccup),[^cite_note-511] [ad-libs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ad-libs), wailings and growls, are all signature to his sound.[^cite_note-Ewing-512][^cite_note-513]

Jackson sang from childhood, and over time his voice and vocal style changed. Between 1971 and 1975, it descended from boy [soprano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soprano) to lyric [tenor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenor).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrackettHoard2004414-514] He was known for his intonation and vocal range.[^cite_note-100_Greatest-466] Through each music release, his vocal development and changes were positively narrated by music journalists. With *Off the Wall* in the late 1970s, Jackson's abilities in his [coming-of-age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coming-of-age) period had *Rolling Stone* comparing his vocals to the "breathless, dreamy stutter" of Stevie Wonder, and wrote that "Jackson's feathery-timbred tenor \[was] extraordinarily beautiful. It slides smoothly into a startling [falsetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falsetto) that's used very daringly."[^cite_note-515] When *Thriller* was released in the early 1980s, *Rolling Stone* wrote that Jackson was singing in a "fully adult voice" that was "tinged by sadness".[^cite_note-Thriller-516] With the release of the introspective album *Dangerous*, *The New York Times* wrote that on some tracks "he gulps for breath, his voice quivers with anxiety or drops to a desperate whisper, hissing through clenched teeth" and he had a "wretched tone". When singing of brotherhood or self-esteem he would return to "smooth" vocals.[^cite_note-NYT_Dangerous-517] Of *Invincible*, *Rolling Stone* wrote that Jackson still performed "exquisitely voiced rhythm tracks and vibrating vocal harmonies".[^cite_note-518] [Joseph Vogel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Vogel_\(author\)) noted Jackson's ability to use non-verbal sounds to express emotion.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVogel20129-519] [Neil McCormick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neil_McCormick) wrote that Jackson's unorthodox singing style "was original and utterly distinctive".[^cite_note-520]

### Influences

Jackson was influenced by musicians including [James Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Brown), [Little Richard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Richard), [Jackie Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackie_Wilson), [Diana Ross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diana_Ross), [Fred Astaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Astaire), [Sammy Davis Jr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sammy_Davis_Jr)., [Gene Kelly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_Kelly),[^cite_note-521] and [David Ruffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ruffin).[^cite_note-522] Little Richard had a substantial influence on Jackson,[^cite_note-523] but Brown was his greatest inspiration. When Jackson was a small child, his mother would wake him whenever Brown appeared on television. Jackson described being "mesmerized".[^cite_note-524]

Jackson's vocal technique was influenced by Diana Ross; his use of the *oooh* interjection from a young age was something Ross had used on many of her songs with [the Supremes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Supremes).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200964-525] She was a mother figure to him, and he often watched her rehearse.[^cite_note-526] He said he had learned from watching how she moved and sang, and that she had encouraged him to have confidence in himself.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200960-527] The choreographer [David Winters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Winters_\(choreographer\)), who met Jackson while choreographing the 1971 Diana Ross TV special *[Diana!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diana!)*, said that Jackson watched the musical *[West Side Story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Side_Story_\(1961_film\))* almost every week and it was his favorite film. Jackson paid tribute to it in the "Beat It" and "Bad" videos.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELewis_Jones20056,_54-528][^cite_note-529][^cite_note-530]

### Musicianship

Jackson had no formal music training and could not read or write [music notation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_notation). He is credited for playing guitar, keyboard, and drums, but was not proficient in them. When composing, he recorded ideas by [beatboxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beatboxing) and imitating instruments vocally.[^cite_note-NME-531] Describing the process, he said: "I'll just sing the bass part into the tape recorder. I'll take that bass [lick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lick_\(music\)) and put the chords of the melody over the bass lick and that's what inspires the melody."[^cite_note-NME-531] The engineer Robert Hoffman recalled Jackson dictating guitar chords note by note and singing string arrangements part by part into a cassette recorder. Jackson's process is demonstrated in his demo for "Beat It".[^cite_note-NME-531]

### Dance

Jackson danced from a young age as part of the Jackson 5,[^cite_note-Sanjoy-532] and incorporated dance extensively in his performances.[^cite_note-Sanjoy-532] According to Sanjoy Roy of *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*, Jackson would "flick and retract his limbs like switchblades, or snap out of a tornado spin into a perfectly poised toe-stand".[^cite_note-Sanjoy-532] The [moonwalk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moonwalk_\(dance\)), taught to him by [Jeffrey Daniel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Daniel),[^cite_note-Daniel-79] was Jackson's signature dance move and one of the most famous of the 20th century.[^cite_note-533] Jackson is credited for coining the name "moonwalk" for this street dance move, previously known as the "backslide".[^cite_note-534][^cite_note-535] His other moves included the [robot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robot_\(dance\)),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMansour2005403-50][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELehman200884-536] crotch grab, and the "anti-gravity" lean of the "Smooth Criminal" video.[^cite_note-Sanjoy-532]

### Themes and genres

Black and white photo of Jackson holding a microphone and singing.

Jackson performing during the Dangerous World Tour in 1992

Jackson explored genres including pop,[^cite_note-allmusic-12][^cite_note-Jet-537] [soul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soul_music),[^cite_note-allmusic-12][^cite_note-Bio2-159] [rhythm and blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhythm_and_blues),[^cite_note-Jet-537] [funk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funk),[^cite_note-Help-538] [rock](25423.md),[^cite_note-Jet-537][^cite_note-Help-538] [disco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disco),[^cite_note-539] [post-disco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-disco),[^cite_note-Help-538] [dance-pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance-pop),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPalmer1995285-540] and [new jack swing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_jack_swing).[^cite_note-allmusic-12] Steve Huey of AllMusic wrote that *Thriller* refined the strengths of *Off the Wall*; the dance and rock tracks were more aggressive, while the pop tunes and ballads were softer and more soulful.[^cite_note-allmusic-12] Its tracks included the ballads "The Lady in My Life", "[Human Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Nature_\(Michael_Jackson_song\))", and "[The Girl Is Mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Girl_Is_Mine)",[^cite_note-Thriller-516][^cite_note-AMThriller-541][^cite_note-Slant-542] the funk pieces "Billie Jean" and "Wanna Be Startin' Somethin'",[^cite_note-Thriller-516][^cite_note-AMThriller-541] and the disco set "[Baby Be Mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baby_Be_Mine_\(Michael_Jackson_song\))" and "[P.Y.T. (Pretty Young Thing)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P.Y.T._\(Pretty_Young_Thing\))".[^cite_note-Slant-542]

With *Off the Wall*, *The Guardian*'s Tom Ewing wrote that Jackson's "vocal tics – the gasps and shudders that punctuate almost every song" evoke "a singer desperate to cut loose and express himself in movement".[^cite_note-Ewing-512] Christopher Connelly of *Rolling Stone* wrote that, with *Thriller*, Jackson developed his long association with the theme of [paranoia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paranoia) and darker imagery.[^cite_note-Thriller-516] AllMusic's [Stephen Thomas Erlewine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Thomas_Erlewine) noted this on the songs "Billie Jean" and "Wanna Be Startin' Somethin'".[^cite_note-AMThriller-541] In "Billie Jean", Jackson depicts an obsessive fan who alleges he has fathered her child,[^cite_note-allmusic-12] and in "Wanna Be Startin' Somethin'" he argues against gossip and the media.[^cite_note-Thriller-516] "Beat It" decried gang violence in a homage to *[West Side Story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Side_Story)*, and was Jackson's first successful rock cross-over piece, according to Huey.[^cite_note-allmusic-12][^cite_note-Bio-41] He observed that "[Thriller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thriller_\(song\))" began Jackson's interest with the theme of the [supernatural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supernatural), a topic he revisited in subsequent years. In 1985, Jackson co-wrote the charity anthem "We Are the World"; humanitarian themes later became a recurring theme in his lyrics and public persona.[^cite_note-allmusic-12]

In *Bad*, Jackson's concept of the predatory lover is seen on the rock song "Dirty Diana".[^cite_note-543] The lead single "I Just Can't Stop Loving You" is a traditional love ballad, and "Man in the Mirror" is a ballad of confession and resolution. "[Smooth Criminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smooth_Criminal)" is an evocation of bloody assault, rape and likely murder.[^cite_note-Time2-135] AllMusic's Stephen Thomas Erlewine states that *Dangerous* presents Jackson as a paradoxical person.[^cite_note-544] The first half of the record is dedicated to new jack swing, including songs like "Jam" and "Remember the Time". It was the first Jackson album in which social ills became a primary theme; "Why You Wanna Trip on Me", for example, protests world hunger, AIDS, homelessness and drugs. *Dangerous* contains sexually charged songs such as "[In the Closet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_the_Closet)". The title track continues the theme of the predatory lover and compulsive desire. The second half includes introspective, pop-gospel anthems such as "[Will You Be There](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_You_Be_There)", "Heal the World", and "Keep the Faith".[^cite_note-NYT_Dangerous-517] In the ballad "[Gone Too Soon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gone_Too_Soon)", Jackson gives tribute to Ryan White and the plight of those with AIDS.[^cite_note-545]

*HIStory* creates an atmosphere of paranoia.[^cite_note-AMHIStory-546] In the new jack swing-funk rock tracks "Scream" and "Tabloid Junkie", and the R\&B ballad "[You Are Not Alone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Are_Not_Alone)", Jackson retaliates against the injustice and isolation he feels, and directs his anger at the media.[^cite_note-RSHIStory-547] In the introspective ballad "[Stranger in Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stranger_in_Moscow)", Jackson laments his "fall from grace"; "Earth Song", "Childhood", "Little Susie", and "Smile" are operatic pop songs.[^cite_note-AMHIStory-546][^cite_note-RSHIStory-547] In "[D.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D.S._\(song\))", Jackson attacks lawyer [Thomas W. Sneddon Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_W._Sneddon_Jr.), who had prosecuted him in both child sexual abuse cases; he describes Sneddon as a white supremacist who wanted to "get my ass, dead or alive".[^cite_note-548] *Invincible* includes urban soul tracks such as "[Cry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cry_\(Michael_Jackson_song\))" and "The Lost Children", ballads such as "[Speechless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speechless_\(Michael_Jackson_song\))", "Break of Dawn", and "Butterflies", and mixes hip-hop, pop, and R\&B in "2000 Watts", "Heartbreaker", and "Invincible".[^cite_note-549][^cite_note-550]

### Music videos and choreography

Jackson released "[Thriller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson's_Thriller_\(music_video\))", a 14-minute music video directed by [John Landis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Landis), in 1983.[^cite_note-551] The [zombie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zombie)-themed video "defined music videos and broke racial barriers" on [MTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV), which had launched two years earlier.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoung200925-44] Before *Thriller*, Jackson struggled to receive coverage on MTV, allegedly because he was African American.[^cite_note-552] Pressure from CBS Records persuaded MTV to start showing "Billie Jean" and later "Beat It", which led to a lengthy partnership with Jackson, and helped other black music artists gain recognition.[^cite_note-Video-553] The popularity of his videos on MTV helped the relatively new channel's viewing figures, and MTV's focus shifted toward pop and R\&B.[^cite_note-Video-553][^cite_note-554] His performance on *Motown 25: Yesterday, Today, Forever* changed the scope of live stage shows, making it acceptable for artists to lip-sync to music video on stage.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInglis2006119,_127-555] The choreography in *Thriller* has been copied in [Indian films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_films) and [prisons in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thriller_\(viral_video\)).[^cite_note-556] *Thriller* marked an increase in scale for music videos, and was named the most successful music video ever by the *Guinness World Records*.[^cite_note-World_Records-240]

In the 19-minute video for "Bad"—directed by [Martin Scorsese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Scorsese)—Jackson used sexual imagery and choreography, and touched his chest, torso and crotch. When asked by Winfrey in the 1993 interview about why he grabbed his crotch, he said it was spontaneously compelled by the music. *Time* magazine described the "Bad" video as "infamous". It featured [Wesley Snipes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesley_Snipes); Jackson's later videos often featured famous cameos.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2009370–373-557][^cite_note-558] For the "Smooth Criminal" video, Jackson experimented with leaning forward at a 45-degree angle, beyond the performer's center of gravity. To accomplish this live, Jackson and designers developed a special shoe to lock the performer's feet to the stage. They were granted [U.S. patent 5,255,452](http://patft.uspto.gov/netacgi/nph-Parser?patentnumber=5255452) for the device.[^cite_note-559] The video for "Leave Me Alone" was not officially released in the US, but in 1989 was nominated for three *Billboard* Music Video Awards[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECampbell1993273-560] and won a Golden Lion Award for its special effects. It won a Grammy for [Best Music Video, Short Form](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Music_Video,_Short_Form).[^cite_note-grammy_mj-61]

He received the [MTV Video Vanguard Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Vanguard_Award) in 1988; in 2001 the award was renamed in his honor.[^cite_note-561] The "Black or White" video simultaneously premiered on November 14, 1991, in 27 countries with an estimated audience of 500 million people, the largest audience ever for a music video at the time.[^cite_note-Achievements-174] Along with Jackson, it featured [Macaulay Culkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macaulay_Culkin), [Peggy Lipton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peggy_Lipton), and [George Wendt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Wendt). It helped introduce [morphing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morphing) to music videos.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECampbell1993303-562] It was controversial for scenes in which Jackson rubs his crotch, vandalizes cars, and throws a garbage can through a storefront. He apologized and removed the final scene of the video.[^cite_note-ew1991-163]

"In the Closet" featured [Naomi Campbell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naomi_Campbell) in a courtship dance with Jackson.[^cite_note-563] "Remember the Time" was set in [ancient Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Egypt), and featured [Eddie Murphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eddie_Murphy), [Iman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iman_\(model\)), and [Magic Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magic_Johnson).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECampbell1993313–314-564] The video for "Scream", directed by [Mark Romanek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Romanek) and production designer Tom Foden, gained a record 11 [MTV Video Music Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award) Nominations, and won "Best Dance Video", "Best Choreography", and "Best Art Direction".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoepple199552-565] The song and its video are Jackson's response to being accused of child molestation in 1993.[^cite_note-566] A year later, it won a Grammy for Best Music Video, Short Form. It has been reported as the [most expensive music video ever made](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_music_videos), at \$7 million;[^cite_note-567] Romanek has contradicted this.[^cite_note-568] The "Earth Song" video was nominated for the 1997 Grammy for Best Music Video, Short Form.[^cite_note-569]

*[Michael Jackson's Ghosts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson's_Ghosts)*, a short film written by Jackson and [Stephen King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_King) and directed by [Stan Winston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_Winston), premiered at the 1996 [Cannes Film Festival](101460.md). At over 38 minutes, it held the Guinness world record for the longest music video until 2013.[^cite_note-570] The 2001 video for "[You Rock My World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Rock_My_World)" lasts over 13 minutes, was directed by [Paul Hunter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Hunter_\(director\)), and features [Chris Tucker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Tucker) and [Marlon Brando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marlon_Brando).[^cite_note-571] It won an [NAACP Image Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NAACP_Image_Award) for Outstanding Music Video in 2002.[^cite_note-572]

In December 2009, the [Library of Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Library_of_Congress) selected "Thriller" as the only music video on the [National Film Registry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Film_Registry), as a work of "enduring importance to American culture".[^cite_note-573][^cite_note-574] Huey wrote that Jackson transformed the music video into an artform and a promotional tool through complex story lines, dance routines, special effects and famous cameos, while breaking down racial barriers.[^cite_note-allmusic-12]

## Awards and honors

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of awards and nominations received by Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Michael_Jackson).



Jackson and President [George H. W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_H._W._Bush) at the White House on April 5, 1990. It was the second time that Jackson had been honored by a US president.

Jackson is one of the [best-selling music artists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_best-selling_music_artists) in history,[^cite_note-575] with worldwide record sales estimated from 500 million to more than 1 billion.[^cite_note-576][^cite_note-577][^cite_note-578]<sup>[\[Note 2\]](#endnote_Note_2)</sup> He had 13 [number-one singles in the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_artists_who_reached_number_one_in_the_United_States#J) over his solo career—more than any other male artist in the Hot 100 era at the time of his death.[^cite_note-579] Jackson is one of only three recording artists who have sold over 100 million records both as solo artists and separately as principal members of a band.[^cite_note-580] He was invited to the [White House](33057.md) three times. In 1984, he was honored with a "Presidential Public Safety Commendation" award by [Ronald Reagan](25433.md) for his humanitarian endeavors.[^cite_note-581] In 1990, he was honored as the "Artist of the Decade", which was awarded by the [National Children's Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Children's_Museum), by [George H. W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_H._W._Bush).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGeorge200444–45-582] In 1992, he was honored as a "Point of Light Ambassador" by Bush for inviting disadvantaged children to his [Neverland Ranch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neverland_Ranch).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECampbell1993321-583]

&#x20;Jackson won hundreds of awards and is one of the most-awarded artists in popular music.[^cite_note-584] His awards include 39 Guinness World Records, including the Most Successful Entertainer of All Time,[^cite_note-CityNews-464][^cite_note-Ditzian-465] 13 [Grammy Awards](44635.md),[^cite_note-585] as well as the [Grammy Legend Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Legend_Award)[^cite_note-586] and the [Grammy Lifetime Achievement Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Lifetime_Achievement_Award),[^cite_note-587] and 26 American Music Awards, including the Artist of the Century and Artist of the 1980s.[^cite_note-MTV-261] He received the [World Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Music_Awards)'s Best Selling Male Artist of the Millennium and the [Bambi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bambi_Award) Pop Artist of the Millennium Award.[^cite_note-CIIC-262][^cite_note-588]



Jackson's star on the [Hollywood Walk of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame) on the occasion of the 15th anniversary of his death

Jackson was inducted onto the [Hollywood Walk of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame) in 1980 as a member of the Jacksons, and in 1984 as a solo artist. He was [inducted to the Rock and Roll Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Rock_and_Roll_Hall_of_Fame_inductees#Performers) and [Vocal Group Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocal_Group_Hall_of_Fame) as a member of the Jackson 5 in 1997 and 1999,[^cite_note-589] respectively, and again as a solo artist in 2001.[^cite_note-590] In 2002, he was added to the [Songwriters Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Songwriters_Hall_of_Fame).[^cite_note-591] In 2010, he became the first recording artist to be inducted into the [Dance Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_Dance_and_Hall_of_Fame),[^cite_note-592] and in 2014, he was posthumously inducted into the [Rhythm and Blues Music Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhythm_and_Blues_Music_Hall_of_Fame).[^cite_note-593] In 2021, he was among the inaugural inductees into the [Black Music & Entertainment Walk of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Music_&_Entertainment_Walk_of_Fame).[^cite_note-594]

In 1988, [Fisk University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fisk_University) honored him with an honorary doctorate.[^cite_note-595] In 1992, he was invested as a [titular king](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Title_of_honour) of [Sanwi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanwi), a traditional African kingdom.[^cite_note-596] In 2009, the Lunar Republic Society named a crater on the Moon after Jackson.[^cite_note-597] In August, for what would have been Jackson's 51st birthday, Google dedicated their [Google Doodle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google_Doodle) to him.[^cite_note-598] In 2012, the extinct hermit crab *[Mesoparapylocheles michaeljacksoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesoparapylocheles_michaeljacksoni)* was named in his honor.[^cite_note-599] In 2014, the [British Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Council) of Cultural Relations deemed Jackson's life one of the 80 most important cultural moments of the 20th century.[^cite_note-600] [World Vitiligo Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Vitiligo_Day) is June 25, the anniversary of Jackson's death, to raise awareness of the auto-immune disorder that Jackson suffered from.[^cite_note-601]

## Earnings

Main article: [Estate of Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estate_of_Michael_Jackson)

In 1989, Jackson's annual earnings from album sales, endorsements, and concerts were estimated at \$125 million.[^cite_note-World_Records-240] *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* placed Jackson's annual income at \$35 million in 1996 and \$20 million in 1997.[^cite_note-602] Jackson has been one of the wealthiest [celebrities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_celebrities_by_net_worth) and [musical artists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_music_artists_by_net_worth); estimates of Jackson's net worth during his life range from negative \$285 million to positive \$350 million for 2002, 2003 and 2007.[^cite_note-603][^cite_note-604] *Forbes* reported in August 2018 that Jackson's total career pretax earnings in life and death were \$4.2 billion.[^cite_note-605][^cite_note-606] Sales of his recordings through Sony's music unit earned him an estimated \$300 million in royalties. He may have earned another \$400 million from concerts, music publishing (including his share of the Beatles catalog), endorsements, merchandising and music videos.[^cite_note-607]

In 2013, the executors of Jackson's estate filed a petition in the [United States Tax Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Tax_Court) as a result of a dispute with the [Internal Revenue Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_Revenue_Service) (IRS) over [estate taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estate_tax_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-608] The executors valued it at about \$7 million, while the IRS valued it at more than \$1.1 billion. In February 2014, the IRS reported that Jackson's estate owed \$505 million in taxes, and \$197 million in penalties.[^cite_note-609] In 2021, the Tax Court issued a ruling in favor of the estate, ruling that the estate's total combined value was \$111.5 million and that the value of Jackson's name and likeness was \$4 million.[^cite_note-610]

In 2016, *Forbes* estimated annual gross earnings by the Jackson estate at \$825 million, the largest ever for a celebrity, mostly due to the sale of the Sony/ATV catalog.[^cite_note-611] In 2018, the figure was \$400 million,[^cite_note-612] and by 2024, it rose to \$600 million,[^cite_note-Forbes-613] bringing Jackson's postmortem total to \$3.3 billion.[^cite_note-Forbes-613] *Forbes* has consistently recognized Jackson as one of the [top-earning dead celebrities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes'_list_of_the_world's_highest-paid_dead_celebrity), placing him at the top spot from 2010 to 2024, except for 2012, 2021, and 2022.[^cite_note-614][^cite_note-615]

In 1987, he left the Jehovah's Witnesses. There was strong opposition to his "Thriller" video on the part of some Witnesses. Perhaps the controversy figured into his decision to leave. But I don't know that for a fact because I didn't talk to him about what he'd done. I couldn't. Witnesses do not discuss spiritual matters with a person who has disassociated himself from the Witnesses, including family members. But I want to stress that, contrary to published reports, I was not required to "shun" my son. Our relationship is as loving today as it was when he was a Witness. I just can't ask him, "Why, Michael?"
